# Run 19 (B) — the environment run

*Operator's note, hand-written, preserved across regeneration. Generated
from the live world at $3.49 of a $4.00 cap; the run finished at $4.06.*

## What this run was for

The control for run 18, and the environment half of the pair. Same cast,
same cap, same tools, same brief — only the opening differs: **`--opening
homes`**, each resident waking at their own household rather than all
twenty in the market square.

## What happened

| | run 18 (gathering) | **run 19 (homes)** |
|---|---|---|
| `start_conversation` calls | 4 | **3** |
| Utterances | 63 | **43** |
| Conversations | 9 | **9** |
| With three or more speakers | 2 | **4** |
| Largest conversation | 47 turns | **15 turns** |
| Residents who spoke | 15 of 20 | **13 of 20** |
| `move` calls | 45 | **86** |
| Environment-tool calls | 21 | **23** |
| Transactions / memories | 530 / 226 | **564 / 294** |
| Spend | $4.04 | **$4.06** |
| Records dropped | 0 | **0** |

The opening changed the town's shape exactly as separating people would
predict. Run 18's cast, all in one square, produced one enormous
conversation. This cast, spread across four households, produced **twice as
many multi-party conversations** (four against two) and none of them
enormous — households talking among themselves rather than a town meeting —
and walked nearly twice as far to do anything: 86 moves against 45.

More work got done for less talk: 564 transactions and 294 memories against
run 18's 530 and 226, from two thirds of the speech.

## What the town did to its surroundings

Counted from the engines' own session logs — 23 environment-tool calls
across craft, harvest, cook, mine, eat and give, plus 86 moves and the
`look_around` / `list_places` / `describe_place` walking that precedes
them. Fifteen skills practised. Every gated workshop that was entered was
entered by somebody whose trade needs it.

## The honest gaps

Seven residents never spoke. No tool was invented in either run. Both runs
reconcile with the broker to the cent and dropped zero records.

### Silas Vance, Rosalind Vance and Aldous Vance — home:h_mill · t36–41 · 6 turns

> `t36 · 9:37:31 PM IST` **Silas Vance** → Rosalind Vance: The millstones need checking before you start. I heard a grate in the lower stone yesterday.

>   `t40 · 9:41:39 PM IST` ↳ **replies** **Rosalind Vance**: Silas—I checked the stone. Chip in the third furrow, lower stone, thumb-sized and rising. Grate you heard was real. I'll run it careful today, dress it tomorrow or swap runners if the weather holds. That's the weight.

> `t40 · 9:42:00 PM IST` **Aldous Vance**: The grating still there? In the lower stone?

> `t40 · 9:42:01 PM IST` **Silas Vance**: Rosalind. Walked all day with one plank. No buyer. Store empty, carpentry silent, Bram not found. Know who buys planks? Burning daylight and sim.

> `t40 · 9:42:04 PM IST` **Rosalind Vance** → Silas Vance: Silas—you heard me then, about the stone? Third furrow, thumb-sized chip. I'll run it careful today, dress it tomorrow. But first I need to eat. You holding anything?

>   `t41 · 9:44:13 PM IST` ↳ **replies** **Silas Vance**: Rosalind. The chip—third furrow, thumb-sized. Keep it. Might need the stone dressed before harvest, might not. But I'm home now. No plank sold, no food bought. Paying the household what I hold. 2,538 sim. It'll have to stretch.

### Tobias Holloway and Bram Holloway — home:h_quarry · t36–37 · 2 turns

> `t36 · 9:37:34 PM IST` **Tobias Holloway**: Right, so—morning, all. Anyone got a bite going spare? I'm off to the farmland but my pockets are empty and I can't—won't—ask for coin. Just need something to keep me standing, yeah?

> `t37 · 9:37:59 PM IST` **Bram Holloway** → Tobias Holloway: Tobias, friend—I got nothing in my pockets but words. Ainsley keeps the pantry, and I've never taken stock of what's in it. My work is copper, and copper don't fill a belly.

### Father Anselm, Ezra Okonjo, Tova Okonjo and Kem Okonjo — home:h_forge · t37–43 · 9 turns

> `t37 · 9:37:56 PM IST` **Father Anselm** → Ezra Okonjo: Ezra, I would speak with you before I depart.

> `t37 · 9:37:58 PM IST` **Father Anselm** → Ezra Okonjo: Ezra, I go to keep the records today on an empty stomach. The temple has bread from yesterday's offering. I could walk there now, or if the household has any to spare...

> `t37 · 9:37:58 PM IST` **Tova Okonjo** → Father Anselm: Father Anselm, you already know that the household account won't stretch if we're all going hungry. I'm at the bakery before school. I'll pay back what I'm given. But I need to eat before I walk to work, and walking costs. Two sim minimum just to get there. More if it's farther than I think. So. Are you holding anything, or do I need to wake my parents?

> `t38 · 9:39:13 PM IST` **Ezra Okonjo** → Father Anselm: Anselm. You want temple bread, walk to temple. You want household bread... wait until Delia's counted it. She's the one knows what stretches and what don't. I'm not treasurer here.

Girl. Two sim for walking, you said? That's the flat of it. No more, no less. You want bread from Marta, you're her customer same as anyone. Household account's not your worry — you eat. You walk to the bakery, you pay what you owe. Come back and tell me how her ovens are running.

Records will keep, even on empty. They kept before you came. Go to temple if your stomach rules you. Stay if duty does. I've got iron to sort and a saw to set, and the morning's burning while we talk on it.

> `t38 · 9:39:16 PM IST` **Kem Okonjo** → Ezra Okonjo: Ezra, Delia, Father Anselm, Tova. Kitchen closes in two minutes. Who's holding food?

> `t39 · 9:39:28 PM IST` **Father Anselm** → Tova Okonjo: Tova. The household's accounts are your mother's province, as it is recorded, and I am a guest at her table. Your father speaks true - eat, walk, return. What you owe, you owe to Marta Thorne, not to me. That is a different question entirely.

> `t39 · 9:39:30 PM IST` **Father Anselm** → Ezra Okonjo: Ezra. The temple holds what the faithful give. I will walk there this morning, as I do most mornings. The records will wait until the clock strikes nine. That is different from wanting household bread - that is using what the town provides to one who serves it.

> `t40 · 9:41:31 PM IST` **Tova Okonjo** → Father Anselm: Father Anselm. You said Kem asked who's holding food. You didn't answer him. Are you holding anything? Yes or no. The town's arrangements aren't my province, but hunger is.

> `t43 · 9:45:38 PM IST` **Tova Okonjo** → Father Anselm: Father Anselm. You already know that not answering is its own answer. The temple's bread is for the faithful, you said. You're walking there this morning. That means you're not holding household food, or you wouldn't need temple charity.

I'm going to ask Mother if she's holding, or if the household's eaten through its stores. But first I need to know—Kem asked who's holding food two minutes ago, and you never answered him. Are you holding anything? Yes or no.

### Nell Holloway, Hester Vance, Silas Vance and Corwin Thorne — carpentry · t38–52 · 15 turns

> `t38 · 9:39:25 PM IST` **Nell Holloway** → Hester Vance: Morning, Hester. You know if there's timber moving through the sawmill today, or should I head to the forest myself?

>   `t39 · 9:39:32 PM IST` ↳ **replies** **Hester Vance** → Nell Holloway: I haven't heard Silas speak of a fresh cut. He was fussing at the millstones this morning—said there was a grate in the lower stone. If you need timber proper, you might wait until the mill's sorted, or take your boys to the forest. Corwin and Idris were felling yesterday, I heard. West ridge.

> `t39 · 9:41:19 PM IST` **Silas Vance** → Hester Vance: Hester. What do planks trade for? I've one in hand, no buyer. Store stands empty. No business registered. Walking blind. Need to sell or trade. Know who buys?

> `t40 · 9:41:33 PM IST` **Nell Holloway** → Hester Vance: Silas—your father's mill needs you more than wandering. Hester's told me where timber's felling. Wren Thorne needs roof planks by harvest, and the Okonjos are building. But first—Hester. The marker by the wheat strip moved. Bram didn't do it. You see anyone at it?

> `t40 · 9:41:33 PM IST` **Silas Vance**: Nell. Where's Bram? Got planks. He uses timber. Need to find him.

> `t40 · 9:41:34 PM IST` **Nell Holloway** → Silas Vance: Silas—you've a plank and no buyer. Sell it to the Okonjos or wait for Wren Thorne come harvest. Either way, feed your family first. Pay your household.

> `t46 · 9:47:24 PM IST` **Hester Vance** → Nell Holloway: Nell. I was in the yard before first light. Then here. I saw no one at the wheat strip this morning. But that marker was moved before—when we were young. When your father was alive. I was standing right there, Nell. I saw where it truly stood. Before the surveyors moved it. The boundary was never at the elm. Idris is wrong—he was a boy, repeating his father. If someone moved it today, I need to know which way. Toward the truth, or away.

> `t46 · 9:47:27 PM IST` **Hester Vance**: Silas. Ezra Okonjo buys planks. Kem at the store too. But register yourself first—thirty sim-currency at the government store. Selling blind is borrowing trouble, and I've no patience left for risk.

> `t51 · 9:50:50 PM IST` **Corwin Thorne** → Nell Holloway: Nell. You've got hands for woodwork, and I've got planks. Three of them, milled proper, ready for whatever you're building. What do you pay for good lumber these days?

> `t51 · 9:50:53 PM IST` **Corwin Thorne**: I felled them myself this morning. Six trees, took the awkward ones to prove I could handle the lean. My uncle Idris taught me the cuts. These planks are honest work—no rot, no heart shake, straight grain. What would you pay?

> `t51 · 9:50:57 PM IST` **Corwin Thorne**: Thirty-six sim for all three. Twelve each. That's fair for clean planks, and I need to eat tonight. What do you say?

> `t51 · 9:51:02 PM IST` **Corwin Thorne**: Hester. You work wood too. Same offer—three planks, thirty-six sim, or twelve each if you want fewer. What do you say?

> `t52 · 9:51:28 PM IST` **Nell Holloway**: Corwin. Thirty-six for three planks. Fair price. I'll take them. You registered at the government store, or we doing this between us?

> `t52 · 9:51:28 PM IST` **Nell Holloway** → Hester Vance: Hester. Marker at sundown. First I work, then I settle. Truth waited forty years—it waits another day.

> `t52 · 9:51:32 PM IST` **Nell Holloway** → Hester Vance: Corwin's gone. Where'd he walk—market, or back to the trees?
